=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Given an array nums, you can perform the following operation any number of times:

Select the adjacent pair with the minimum sum in nums. If multiple such pairs exist, choose the leftmost one.
Replace the pair with their sum.

Return the minimum number of operations needed to make the array non-decreasing.
An array is said to be non-decreasing if each element is greater than or equal to its previous element (if it exists).
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [5,2,3,1]
Output: 2
Explanation:

The pair (3,1) has the minimum sum of 4. After replacement, nums = [5,2,4].
The pair (2,4) has the minimum sum of 6. After replacement, nums = [5,6].

The array nums became non-decreasing in two operations.

Example 2:

Input: nums = [1,2,2]
Output: 0
Explanation:
The array nums is already sorted.

 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 50
-1000 <= nums[i] <= 1000

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minimumPairRemoval(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

We can output:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minimumPairRemoval(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        def sorted_non_decreasing(arr):
            return all(arr[i] <= arr[i+1] for i in range(len(arr)-1))
        
        ops = 0
        while len(nums) > 1 and not sorted_non_decreasing(nums):
            min_idx = 0
            min_sum = nums[0] + nums[1]
            for i in range(1, len(nums)-1):
                s = nums[i] + nums[i+1]
                if s < min_sum:
                    min_sum = s
                    min_idx = i
            nums[min_idx:min_idx+2] = [min_sum]
            ops += 1
        return ops
```

Let's maybe write a more robust solution with a while loop:
```
class Solution:
    def minimumPairRemoval(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        ans = 0
        while any(nums[i] > nums[i+1] for i in range(len(nums)-1)):
            best = 0
            best_sum = nums[0] + nums[1]
            for i in range(1, len(nums)-1):
                s = nums[i] + nums[i+1]
                if s < best_sum:
                    best_sum = s
                    best = i
            nums[best:best+2] = [best_sum]
            ans += 1
        return ans
```
This is concise. Need ensure if len(nums)==1, any over empty range is False, loop not entered. Good. If len(nums)==0? Constraints >=1. Good.

Potential issue: `any(nums[i] > nums[i+1] for i in range(len(nums)-1))` returns True if any inversion. If sorted non-decreasing, no inversion. Good.

Let's think about "non-decreasing" with equal allowed. Inversion is >. Good.

Now, let's consider if the operation "Select the adjacent pair with the minimum sum" should consider the sum of pair after replacement? No.

Loop: any(-1 > -5 True) yes. best_sum=-6, best=0. Merge -> [-6]. ans=2.
Loop: any(empty) False. return 2. Good.

Now, if tie leftmost:

Loop: any(3>1 True) yes. best_sum=4, best=0. Merge -> [4]. ans=2. Good.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minimumPairRemoval(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        ans = 0
        while any(nums[i] > nums[i+1] for i in range(len(nums) - 1)):
            best = 0
            best_sum = nums[0] + nums[1]
            for i in range(1, len(nums) - 1):
                s = nums[i] + nums[i+1]
                if s < best_sum:
                    best_sum = s
                    best = i
            nums[best:best+2] = [best_sum]
            ans += 1
        return ans
```

Let's decide final code. Use helper for clarity? The final answer should be code only. I'd include:
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minimumPairRemoval(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        def is_non_decreasing(arr: List[int]) -> bool:
            return all(arr[i] <= arr[i + 1] for i in range(len(arr) - 1))
        
        ops = 0
        while not is_non_decreasing(nums):
            min_idx = 0
            min_sum = nums[0] + nums[1]
            for i in range(1, len(nums) - 1):
                s = nums[i] + nums[i + 1]
                if s < min_sum:
                    min_sum = s
                    min_idx = i
            
            nums[min_idx:min_idx + 2] = [min_sum]
            ops += 1
        
        return ops
```
This is robust. For len=1, `is_non_decreasing` returns True, loop not entered. For len=0? not possible. Good.

Let's think about if `nums` is modified in place. The method returns int. LeetCode doesn't care if input modified. Fine.

Now, let's consider if there is any hidden "minimum number" if you can choose to perform operations on a sorted array and then it becomes unsorted, but you
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minimumPairRemoval(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        def is_non_decreasing(arr: List[int]) -> bool:
            return all(arr[i] <= arr[i + 1] for i in range(len(arr) - 1))

        ops = 0

        while not is_non_decreasing(nums):
            min_idx = 0
            min_sum = nums[0] + nums[1]

            for i in range(1, len(nums) - 1):
                cur_sum = nums[i] + nums[i + 1]
                if cur_sum < min_sum:
                    min_sum = cur_sum
                    min_idx = i

            nums[min_idx:min_idx + 2] = [min_sum]
            ops += 1

        return ops
```
